CHAPTER 10


Advanced H.264 video transport
CHAPTER 10


Encode, transport, decode,
and present live video
  SOURCE TRAIL --- ANALYZING NOW

  File: Cerebro/Cerebro/ROBCameraH264Encoder.swift
  Why it is here: Follow one frame through ROBVideoServer.swift and ROBVideoProtocol.swift; then cross
  repositories into ROBControllerVision’s H264SampleBufferFactory.swift, H264VideoReceiver.swift, transport
  decoder, and display-layer bridge.

This chapter consolidates the robot-side and Vision Pro books into one advanced three-feed media path. It de-
scribes the implemented H.264 profile and mixed-immersive panoramic presentation, not a generic H.264 tutorial
and not a promise of stereoscopic spatial video, audio transport, HEVC, or unreliable datagrams. Dataset recording
is a separate local subsystem described later.



The end-to-end frame pipeline
                                  CMSampleBuffer    NV12 + letterbox   VideoToolbox H.264




                                  sample renderer   stream validator      RVID / RBVD




Camera capture produces a Core Media CMSampleBuffer containing a pixel buffer and timing. Cerebro retains only
the newest offered raw sample while server work is pending. An accepted, authenticated per-camera subscription
creates an encoder at the negotiated even width/height, frame rate, and bitrate. Front/belly ceilings are 960 by 540;
Insta360 is 960 by 480; all use 20 frames/s and 1.5 Mbit/s ceilings.



Scale into the encoder's pixel format
ROBCameraH264Encoder creates a VTCompressionSession whose destination buffers use video-range bi-planar 4:2:0
YCbCr (NV12). A reusable VTPixelTransferSession scales the source with letterboxing so aspect ratio is preserved.
The compression-session pool supplies destination buffers; allocation uses a threshold of six so pressure becomes
a dropped frame rather than unbounded buffer growth.
At 20 fps, the target interval is 50 ms. Samples arriving sooner are intentionally skipped before conversion. A
dropped allocation or compression result requests a future recovery key frame. Every completion object can be
claimed once, preventing synchronous drop flags and an asynchronous VideoToolbox callback from delivering the
same logical completion twice.



Configure low-latency H.264
The encoder requests real-time operation, disables frame reordering, prefers constrained baseline profile when
supported, supplies expected frame rate and average bitrate, and limits the key-frame interval to approximately

                                                          47
BUILDING R.O.B.                                                                                     COMPLETE FIELD MANUAL - ADVANCED


one second. Disabling reordering means this initial stream does not depend on B-frames; decode timestamps can
remain invalid and presentation order follows the reliable stream.
Each encoded result owns copied bytes rather than a borrowed VideoToolbox buffer. The encoder records a mono-
tonically increasing access-unit sequence, capture time in Unix milliseconds, presentation time and duration in mi-
croseconds, synchronization/key-frame state, NAL-length width, SPS/PPS parameter sets when present, and one
complete AVCC access-unit payload.



Understand AVCC, SPS, PPS, and access units
H.264 consists of Network Abstraction Layer (NAL) units. Annex-B streams separate NAL units with start codes such
as 00 00 00 01. This implementation does not transmit Annex-B. AVCC stores a big-endian byte length before each
NAL unit. The receiver must use the negotiated length-field width; it must not search for start-code patterns inside
compressed data.
The Sequence Parameter Set (SPS) describes coded properties needed to construct a decoder format. The Picture
Parameter Set (PPS) supplies picture-level decoding parameters. Cerebro sends them in a codec-configuration mes-
sage with a positive generation before access units using that generation. A new configuration, encoder recovery,
or requested key frame resends the configuration. An IDR key frame lets the decoder restart without depending on
earlier reference pictures.

   ADVANCED CHANNEL

   Codec configuration is state, not decoration. A payload can be valid H.264 bytes yet undecodable with the
   wrong SPS/PPS, NAL-length width, dimensions, stream ID, or generation. Validate all of them before asking
   Core Media to allocate decoder state.



Wrap media in two bounded layers
The outer ordered-stream frame is RVID version 1. Its 32-byte header carries magic, version, header length, mes-
sage kind, bounded payload length, monotonic connection sequence, and reserved-zero fields. Authentication,
capability, subscription, feedback, configuration, access-unit, and end messages receive distinct kinds.
Encoded configuration and access units use an inner RBVD version-1 header of 92 bytes. It binds media to ses-
sion UUID, subscription UUID, media sequence, capture time, presentation time, duration, timescale, codec-
configuration generation, NAL-length width, flags, and payload length. Integers are network byte order. Current
hard payload limits are 64 KiB for codec configuration and 2 MiB for one access unit; control JSON is capped at 64
KiB and rejects unknown fields.

                                   PRIMARY CHECKS          WHY IT EXISTS
  BOUNDARY
                                   pinned      identity,   Authentic peer and protected ordered bytes.
  TLS/QUIC                         proof, role
                                   magic,      version,    Incremental connection framing before allocation.
  RVID                             kind, length, se-
                                   quence
                                   IDs, codec, timing,     Media belongs to the negotiated live stream.
  RBVD                             generation, lengths
                                   NAL lengths and         Compressed payload can be reconstructed safely.
  AVCC                             configuration
                                   coded/presentation      SPS/PPS cannot silently exceed negotiated geometry.
  Core Media                       dimensions



Apply backpressure before latency accumulates
ORBITUS ROBOTICS - FIRST MAKER FAIRE EDITION                                                                             48 / 220
BUILDING R.O.B.                                                                          COMPLETE FIELD MANUAL - ADVANCED



Video has its own listener, connections, queue, encoder, authentication domain, and send state. It never shares
the control message queue. Cerebro permits only one pending raw source frame, one pending encoder output
per subscription, and one media send in flight. If encoding or send work is occupied, newer unencoded work is
dropped rather than queued. One actual encoded drop or undeliverable output forces a later IDR; replacing a
not-yet-encoded raw frame does not break decoder references.
Every RVID send has a ten-second completion deadline. A newly accepted stream has 15 seconds to produce its
first frame, and a running stream may not remain silent beyond 15 seconds. A peer that stops reading loses the
stream instead of retaining unlimited memory. Three simultaneous maximum-rate feeds can consume roughly 4.5
Mbit/s per viewer plus protocol overhead, so data-plane separation prevents queue coupling but not shared-radio
contention.



Decode framing before decoding H.264
On Vision Pro, the Network.framework adapter first reconstructs complete RVID frames from arbitrary receive chunks.
It rejects invalid magic, version, reserved bytes, payload sizes, and ordering before decoding JSON or RBVD. The
media decoder then checks session ID, subscription ID, codec, configuration generation, sequence, timing, AVCC
length width, and size. These checks occur before creating Core Media state.
H264SampleBufferFactory sorts and extracts SPS/PPS, copies their bytes into temporary stable pointers, and calls
CMVideoFormatDescriptionCreateFromH264ParameterSets. It verifies that coded dimensions remain within mac-
roblock rounding of the negotiated size and that clean-aperture presentation dimensions match. This prevents
hostile or corrupted parameter sets from expanding the decoder beyond the agreed geometry.
For each accepted access unit, the factory allocates a CMBlockBuffer, copies the bounded AVCC payload, creates
timing, and asks Core Media to create a ready sample buffer. The not-sync attachment mirrors the key-frame flag.
The display-immediately attachment requests low-latency presentation rather than building a long presentation
queue.



Present and recover without blocking control
The actor-owned H264VideoReceiver feeds an AVSampleBufferVideoRenderer obtained from the UI’s display layer. If
the renderer is temporarily full, it waits only about 50 ms in short cancellable intervals. Continued pressure drops
the access unit and begins recovery rather than accumulating frames.
Sequence gaps, missing configuration, generation mismatch, decoder failure, or requiresFlushToResumeDecoding
make the validator require a key frame. Recovery flushes decoder presentation state, rate-limits key-frame feedback
to no faster than 500 ms, and discards dependent frames until a valid key frame arrives. A configuration-generation
change also flushes before accepting the new decoder state. Statistics report received, rendered, dropped, bytes,
last sequence, and key-frame requests at a bounded update rate.



Timing, reliability, and tradeoffs
Capture wall-clock time measures end-to-end age. Presentation timestamps provide an internal monotonic media
timeline. Duration is derived from the negotiated frame rate. These clocks answer different questions and should
not be substituted for one another.
Ordered reliable QUIC simplifies configuration and access-unit ordering, but packet loss can delay later video
bytes through head-of-line blocking. Because control uses another QUIC connection, video loss does not queue
a stop command, although both flows share Wi-Fi airtime. Future QUIC datagrams would trade retransmission for
application-managed loss recovery and require a new negotiated contract; they are not silently enabled by the
current enum.




ORBITUS ROBOTICS - FIRST MAKER FAIRE EDITION                                                                  49 / 220
BUILDING R.O.B.                                                                         COMPLETE FIELD MANUAL - ADVANCED



Advanced verification matrix
Test the media pipeline with synthetic frames before live hardware:

• verify scaling, even dimensions, NV12 allocation limits, bitrate, frame-rate skipping, IDR interval, and SPS/PPS
  extraction;
• reject truncated, oversized, mis-versioned, wrong-session, wrong-generation, unknown-field, and out-of-order
  messages before allocation;
• inject access-unit loss and confirm one bounded recovery sequence and key-frame request;
• change codec generation and confirm flush, reconfiguration, and key-frame resume;
• stall the renderer and network independently and prove memory remains bounded;
• close or revoke the control session and prove its video subscription ends;
• measure capture-to-display age, encoded size, drops, recovery time, CPU/GPU use, thermal state, and control
  latency concurrently;
• repeat camera unplug, app background, viewer close, and shutdown while a send and encode callback are in
  flight.

Volume 7 explains the Swift/visionOS client composition, three flat feeds, and inward-facing mixed-immersive
panorama with -90-degree verified start heading and operator rotation/reset controls. Volume 8 explains Cerebro’s
broader camera and model scheduling. This chapter is the shared wire-level and Core Media reference.




ORBITUS ROBOTICS - FIRST MAKER FAIRE EDITION                                                                 50 / 220
CHAPTER 11


Cameras, depth, lidar, and
coordinate frames
CHAPTER 11


Perception and mapping
  SOURCE TRAIL --- ANALYZING NOW

  File: Cerebro/Cerebro/CameraManager.swift
  Why it is here: Camera ownership starts here. Depth helpers remain in Cerebro; lidar transport and fixtures
  live in the separate RPLidar repository named in the repository source map.




RGB-D camera
  EXPERIMENTAL / OPTIONAL The current camera design runs one pinned DepthAI helper for the face camera and another
for the belly camera. Each sends timestamp-synchronized RGB plus depth through its own private local Unix socket.
RGB enters the sample-buffer, Vision, Gemini, recording, and video paths. Depth is little-endian UInt16 millimeters
aligned to RGB; zero is invalid. AVFoundation remains an RGB-only fallback. A third headless Insta360 service owns
camera control, heartbeat, RTMP/ffmpeg preview, demand-driven decode, and six-sector panoramic perception.
The builder identifies an OAK-D Pro Wide in the belly/body and an autofocus OAK-D Pro at the face/head. Record
intended role, MXID, socket, product, board, revision, sensor names, calibration, USB speed, focus capability, and
firmware because OAK variants differ in color shutter/focus/FOV while sharing similar enclosures. Luxonis docu-
ments the RVC2 Pro Wide family with global-shutter monochrome stereo, active IR projection/flood illumination,
USB 2/3, and an onboard IMU. Active illumination and full inference can raise peak power; use a reviewed powered
path rather than depending on a marginal hub.
Each helper owns exactly one selected OAK exclusively. Old frames are dropped when perception is busy. Missing
hardware, wrong role/MXID, malformed IPC, SDK failure, or helper exit becomes per-camera state and bounded
retry rather than a control-process crash.
The helper uses DepthAI Camera, Depth, and Sync nodes, requests the selected profile (1280 by 720 default or
640 by 400 for the configurable main camera), aligns RAW16 millimeter depth to RGB, and emits a versioned CDP1
packet over a private Unix socket. Cerebro validates requested, observed, and encoded dimensions separately,
plus pixel formats, byte counts, and maximum allocation before constructing a CoreVideo buffer. The process
boundary contains SDK and USB failure; a future native or XPC helper should preserve the same CameraFrameSet
provider contract.



Perception snapshots and bounded rendering
Camera rate is not inference rate. Preview may consume the newest RGB frame while Vision pose detection, col-
ored depth, point-cloud geometry, Gemini JPEG sampling, MLX VLM sampling, and H.264 encoding run at distinct
bounded rates. Slow consumers use latest-value mailboxes. SceneKit reuses or replaces named node roots instead
of appending forever; Core Image contexts and pixel-buffer pools are reused; model containers stay actor-owned.
Profile repeated source toggles and 30-minute runs, not one still frame. Look for retained CMSampleBuffer ob-
jects, per-frame CIContext construction, ever-growing SceneKit textures or nodes, missing autorelease pools on
Objective-C worker threads, duplicate model loads, and repeated diagnostics overwhelming the main thread.



Kinect, OpenNI, NiTE, and PCL archaeology
  LEGACY   The ROBNiTEManager, TaskControllers, FreenectPCL, k2g, viewer, shader, serialization, and duplicated
“2” files preserve an older Kinect/OpenNI/NiTE/PCL depth stack. Archived v2 commits date the first surviving

                                                        52
BUILDING R.O.B.                                                                             COMPLETE FIELD MANUAL - ADVANCED


OpenNI/PCL/NiTE sequence to 12 January 2018, with further libfreenect work in May 2018 and continuing percep-
tion experiments in the v3 history during 2019. The files entered the fresh v5 Git history in the 2025 import, but were
not created by it. They are useful evidence for coordinate and visualization experiments, not the recommended
OAK setup.
Before removal, check Xcode target membership, runtime selectors, duplicated model provenance, and any co-
ordinate knowledge that should move into tests or documentation. The modern DepthAI helper improves failure
containment by placing the camera SDK and USB ownership outside the control process.



RPLidar
  EXPERIMENTAL / OPTIONAL The companion app reads pose, location, laser points, and occupancy maps from Slamware.
It publishes scans about every 0.2 seconds and maps about every 5 seconds through typed, sequenced, bounded
telemetry. Compact RLS1 scans carry a 68-byte header plus four bytes per valid bounded point (8–8192 points,
0.03–30 m); 720 points occupy 2948 bytes, about 14.7 KB/s at 5 Hz. The old sub-meter front/back filter is gone. The
occupancy map remains a separate payload. The publisher role cannot drive the robot.
The App Group ID is group.com.orbitusrobotics.rob. Same-Mac publication prefers its Unix-domain IPC with
per-sample HMAC, role, revocation, sequence, and freshness checks; paired QUIC is the automatic fallback.
This requires matching App Group provisioning. The mapping app aligns updates with scan-captured pose,
saves/loads/resets .robomap, and overlays maps on OSM with persisted 50–150 percent scale and -180–180 degree
north rotation. North is manual unless a trusted heading source exists.
The RPLidar was a later mechanical stage supported by three 3/8-inch threaded rods. The builder reports Ether-
net descending to a base USB hub through a USB–Ethernet adapter; the Arduino uses the same hub for its USB
serial path. Confirm the exact RPLidar model because SLAMTEC variants differ between native USB/serial and Eth-
ernet interfaces. Measure the lidar-to-base transform, mast deflection, vibration, rod preload/locking, laser plane
clearance, and cable strain through the full flipper, waist, arm, and transport envelopes.
Cerebro checks authenticated publisher identity, monotonic sequence, capture time, kind, and bounds. Stale data
is rejected. Social roam and destination autonomy consume only the fresh sensor products their policies name;
occupancy/map display never grants motion authority.



IR and IMU
  EXPERIMENTAL / OPTIONALSix Sharp IR sensors are installed. Front/back warnings remain active, but automatic inhibit
assignments are disabled and full numeric telemetry is not emitted by the current firmware. Grass repeatedly pro-
duced false positives and stopped useful travel during earlier experiments. A future Cerebro policy can consume
all six values only after the firmware publishes structured samples with age and quality; filter/debounce them, cross-
check lidar and commanded direction, distinguish warning from safety authority, and fail to “unknown” on silence.
                      The MPU9250 code reports inertial data and derived orientation, but the builder reports that
  EXPERIMENTAL / OPTIONAL
the physical long I C wires were too noisy inside ROB’s high-EMF environment to be useful. It is not a motion-inhibit
                            2

input. Shorter local wiring, careful return routing, shielding/ground strategy, lower bus speed where appropriate,
or a local SPI/CAN sensor node should be tested before restoring the signal.



Coordinate-frame discipline
Name every transform:

• lidar sensor to base;
• base to local map;
• color camera to depth camera alignment;
• camera to head/neck;

• head/neck to torso;
• torso to each arm base;
• arm joint chain to gripper tool frame.

Record axis direction, handedness, units, timestamp, calibration method, uncertainty, and valid motion range. A
visually plausible transform can still command a gripper to the wrong side of an object.

Servo and arm control
Cerebro uses Maestro servo channels plus AMBER Python paths. ROB’s present AMBER evidence describes two
seven-joint B1 arms and separate grippers. The Ubuntu-side directories L-10 and R-11 bind can10/can11, UDP ports
26001/26002, and LCM prefixes Left_/Right_. Network clients send UDP or LCM to one core per side; each core,
not the client, interfaces with its actuators over CAN.

                                                                                             120 Ω at physical end∗                                                              120 Ω at physical end∗




                            USB        USB serial / SLCAN                                                                                 twisted pair
           Ubuntu host                                                  CAN transceiver                                                                                                                     AMBER arm nodes
                                            adapter                                                                                     CANH + CANL
            SocketCAN                                               TXD/RXD ↔ differential                                                                                                                  actuator electronics
                                                                                                                                        reference GND




                                                                                                    CANH and CANL carry one differential signal. GND is a reference path, not a third data wire.
                                                                                        Connector pin numbers, isolation, shield bonding, and built-in termination must be verified on the installed adapter.


∗
    Conceptual high-speed CAN bus. Two 120 Ω end terminations usually measure about 60 Ω across CANH–CANL with all power removed. ROB uses two separate
                                                              buses: can10 left and can11 right.


The adapter connector must expose or be mapped to CANH, CANL, and the appropriate reference ground. High-
speed CAN uses a twisted pair with 120 Ω termination at the two physical ends; two correct end terminators normally
appear as roughly 60 Ω across CANH–CANL with power removed. Verify whether each USB adapter or arm controller
has switchable/internal termination before adding any resistor. ROB has two independent buses, so perform the
measurement and side-identity audit separately for can10 and can11.
The rear switches behind the arms are reported to interrupt the 48 V arm supply. Document whether each switch
removes one arm or a shared converter path, its DC contact rating, arc suppression, connector/fuse order, behavior
under regenerative or held loads, and how the operator proves isolation. A switch label does not establish an
emergency-stop category.
The checked-in V2 protocol provides status, joint and Cartesian moves, calibration, gripper control, and mode
control. Its packed UDP header contains a 16-bit command, 16-bit length, and 32-bit counter. The current client
needs stronger validation of source, size, command, counter, freshness, finite values, and authentication before
production use. Archived binaries do not include enough source to reconstruct their actuator-level CAN protocol.
The hardened Ubuntu gateway now provides that stronger host boundary: a root-owned initializer maps reviewed
USB serials to can10/can11; a systemd service runs unprivileged; LCM status is decoded into locked snapshots; one
authenticated controller session owns bounded per-arm queues; heartbeat and leased commands expire; UDP
replies are matched by command and counter; and priority hold purges queued work. Volume 6’s appended com-
pendium walks through every layer and the service file line by line.


                                                                             56
BUILDING R.O.B.                                                                             COMPLETE FIELD MANUAL - ADVANCED


URDF models provide link geometry, joint origins, axes, and limits, but they do not prove ROB’s physical mount
transforms, encoder zero, payload, safe stopping, or cable collision volume. Measure torso-to-left-base, torso-to-
right-base, and each tool transform; then verify forward kinematics over several supported poses before inverse
kinematics. Volume 6 contains the protocol tables, joint model, Ubuntu reproduction procedure, and staged dual-
arm commissioning gates.



Keyframe animation
  IMPLEMENTED  KeyframeAnimationManager.swift persists JSON values for arm channels, head/neck, tread movement,
flipper, actuator, torso rotation, and speech. Keyframes are reusable data: a sequence can describe expressive
behavior independently of the UI that recorded it.
  COMMANDED  Historical servo “animation” helpers in ROBSerialBox.m do not yet perform robust smooth interpolation;
they update their targets immediately. New interpolation must respect joint rates, synchronization, limits, collisions,
and cancellation.



Speech
  IMPLEMENTED Cerebro configures an Apple local speech-recognition path and uses local speech synthesis for com-
pleted responses. This path reduces network dependence, but it does not describe every microphone use: when
the separately optional Gemini session is enabled, microphone audio may be streamed to an external cloud service.
Both paths require microphone permissions, public notice, and a plan for unintended capture.



Consent-based face identity
  EXPERIMENTAL / OPTIONAL Face identity defaults off and remains local. Enrollment requires a paired, non-revoked opera-
tor plus local confirmation and collects 24 varied samples. Vision performs face/landmark/quality gates; recognition
adds open-set distance, runner-up margin, and temporal consensus. Opaque UUID profiles encrypt metadata, em-
beddings, and retained JPEGs with AES-GCM and a device-only Keychain key; whole-profile deletion is available.
A recognized name enters untrusted scene context for 15 seconds and never grants motion, shell, secrets, action
approval, Messages administration, or any other authority.
Two AdaFace IR18 Core ML encoders are selectable. WebFace4M is the recommended default; VGGFace2 is
an optional comparison. Both use 112-by-112 BGR normalized by pixel * (2/255) - 1 and return 512-value L2-
normalized embeddings. Profiles store their encoder ID and cross-model comparisons are forbidden. The current
0.35 maximum cosine distance and 0.06 margin require on-ROB false-accept/reject, lookalike, lighting, pose, oc-
clusion, and replay calibration. Liveness/depth remains future work. The conversion script validates PyTorch/Core
ML agreement and hashes local checkpoints; weights stay out of Git.



Private Messages bridge
 EXPERIMENTAL / OPTIONAL The bridge defaults off and uses Full Disk Access for query-only reads of Messages chat.db;
Automation permission is needed only to reply. Exact sender allowlists, a persisted high-water mark, at-most-
once processing, one-to-one/account checks, and a five-per-minute global rate limit fail closed on groups, out-
going/reaction/partial/stale items, and unexpected accounts.
Each allowed chat has an isolated AI session with no room speech/transcript, motor/action, Music, arbitrary-file,
device, camera, or microphone tools. One JPEG/PNG/HEIC/HEIF attachment up to 10 MB/24 MP can be normalized
without metadata to at most 2048 px. Gemini may receive it only when configured; otherwise Swift MLX receives
pixels and Apple Foundation Models receives bounded textual visual analysis. Fixed-publisher news and Open-
Meteo weather with explicit location are allowed; Mac location and caller-supplied URLs are not.



ORBITUS ROBOTICS - FIRST MAKER FAIRE EDITION                                                                     57 / 220
BUILDING R.O.B.                                                                               COMPLETE FIELD MANUAL - ADVANCED


Optional transcript memory defaults off. SQLite fields are AES-GCM encrypted, exact-match indexes use HMAC,
and a 256-bit device-only Keychain key plus owner-only permissions protect the store. Retrieval remains bounded
to the same canonical sender/account; Gemini use discloses selected text excerpts off-Mac, never stored image
pixels. Search, plaintext export, and clear-all controls require an explicit retention/consent policy.
 Exact locally configured administrator messages bypass AI and may request Shutdown/Reboot only after exact YES
 from the same sender/chat within 90 seconds. Confirmation is one-shot. Reviewed scripts run with fixed /bin/zsh
-f -s, arrive through stdin, and time out after 30 seconds; message text is never interpolated. Display a critical local
warning and never publish real handles/scripts.



Explicit recording and dataset provenance
  EXPERIMENTAL / OPTIONAL Recording starts only through an explicit operator action. Recoverable append-only ses-
sions synchronize face/belly RGB keyframes, aligned lossless depth, stereo, calibration, exact .rscan lidar, local
pose/odometry, tread/authority, traversability labels, and separate face/belly/Insta360 movies. Requested, ob-
served, and encoded geometry are distinct facts. Autonomous commands are logged but never label themselves;
belly traversability becomes a candidate label only after manual traversal plus odometry confirmation. Capacity,
consent, retention, deletion/export, dataset splits, and model promotion require human governance.



Controller-activated autonomy
                        The social-roam profile begins only after explicit controller authorization, captures an activa-
  EXPERIMENTAL / OPTIONAL
tion pose and radius, uses fresh bounded lidar scans, and limits tread speed. Near 82 percent of the configured
radius it attempts to return toward its origin; this is not verified containment. Missing or stale lidar requests a motion
stop and transitions to waitingForLidar while the autonomy session remains active. Manual takeover, explicit stop,
expiry, replacement, or shutdown ends the session. Local deterministic code owns execution.
Controller destination navigation is a distinct deterministic path: ROBController performs Nominatim search, Cere-
bro uses Valhalla planning, a current pilot limits requests to 50 m, and stale pose/map/lidar/depth/authority gates
reject or stop the route. Myriad X sidewalk segmentation provides bounded centerline/confidence context at about
5 Hz. Neither a Messages request, face identity, VLM label, nor Gemini prose can open tread authority.



Gemini integration
                      The entire Gemini integration defaults off and requires explicit enablement plus credentials.
  EXPERIMENTAL / OPTIONAL
Once enabled, the current configuration defaults camera-frame and microphone-audio streaming on unless each
stream is separately disabled. The robot-action tool remains separately off by default. Its schema declares look_at,
play_gesture, request_pick, navigate_relative, and stop_motion; the model never receives arbitrary raw serial out-
put. Only stop_motion has an immediate local executor, and even that returns a partial result because arm hold is
unverified. The other actions have no deterministic executor: the controller console records approval or rejection
but does not actuate hardware.
  PLANNED   General picking is intentionally unavailable. Missing prerequisites include calibrated camera-to-arm
transforms, complete neck mapping, joint feedback, inverse kinematics, collision checking, grasp planning, and
a verified executor.

   File: Cerebro/Cerebro/ROBStageShowCoordinator.swift
   Why it is here:         Read the strict show protocol and fixture tests first, then compare
   ROBMLXImprovisationProvider.swift, ROBSaberChoreography.swift, and the JSON catalog under StageShows.


The stage-show coordinator is a cancellable state machine rather than a sequence of arbitrary shell or motor com-
mands. Show JSON may request speech, waits, checkpoints, allow-listed gestures, and adaptive dialogue; it may
not contain raw joints, hosts, ports, or shell commands. Dry Run has no side effects. Offline uses authored lines.
Local mode may use a schema-validated MLX or loopback llama.cpp line. Adaptive mode may give a trusted local
plan to Gemini, but timeout and failure fall back through validated local text to mandatory authored text. Late model
completions are correlated and discarded.
The comedy show demonstrates why fallback belongs to creative design: roughly ten minutes of timing cannot
depend on one network or model response. Progressive saber training maps named profiles to bounded Carte-
sian transforms and duration. It must remain theatrical, slow, guarded choreography with soft props, a participant
boundary, dry-run visualization, independent stop, transform, inverse-kinematics, and collision validation, and no
autonomous contact. Vision may score timing or offer spoken coaching; pixels do not directly command the AMBER
arms.

CHAPTER 13


Verification before motion
CHAPTER 13


Test strategy and safety case
Requirements traceability
Every test should trace to a requirement and hazard. A simple matrix includes requirement ID, hazard ID, source
revision, test method, equipment, pass criteria, result, evidence path, reviewer, and date. “It worked once” is not a
repeatable acceptance criterion.



Progressive test ladder
             SCOPE                                                REQUIRED EVIDENCE
 LEVEL
             Design review and hazard analysis.                   Reviewed drawings, calculations, assumptions,
 0                                                                and unresolved risks.
             Pure software fixtures and simulation.               Protocol, range, state, deadline, replay, and
 1                                                                cleanup tests.
             Logic outputs to indicators or dummy loads.          Pin states, ordering, safe initialization, timeout
 2                                                                waveform.
             One controller with a small surrogate or discon-     Polarity, limits, current, fault and stop response.
 3           nected actuator.
             Full hardware on rated stands with guards.           Direction, speed cap, coast/brake, current, tem-
 4                                                                perature, E-stop timing.
             Slow unloaded floor test in an exclusion zone.       Tracking, latency, stopping distance, spotter log,
 5                                                                post-test inspection.
             Incremental loads and declared surfaces.             Repeatable operating envelope and ther-
 6                                                                mal/current margins.
             Full show rehearsal.                                 Setup checklist, barrier, staffing, failure drills, pri-
 7                                                                vacy notice, teardown plan.



Failure injection
Test the robot’s response when:

• joystick input stops or becomes non-finite;
• Watch-to-phone relay disappears;
• controller packets duplicate, reorder, age, or exceed bounds;
• control network disappears while video continues;
• video decoder stalls or requests a keyframe;
• USB serial is unplugged or filled with malformed bytes;
• Arduino boots with IMU missing;
• an IR sensor sticks low or produces grass reflections;
• lidar data becomes stale or claims an impossible timestamp;
• DepthAI helper exits or the camera disconnects;


                                                           63
BUILDING R.O.B.                                                                             COMPLETE FIELD MANUAL - ADVANCED


• an actuator reaches a mechanical stop;
• one motor direction is reversed;
• a credential is revoked during active control;
• phone and Vision clients request control simultaneously or the prior owner continues sending after takeover;
• either arm grip releases, arm telemetry becomes stale, or one per-arm authority expires;
• one of three camera roles reports the wrong MXID/socket or disconnects while the other feeds continue;
• a face comparison uses the wrong encoder, a replay image, a lookalike, corrupt ciphertext, or a missing gallery
  key;
• Messages supplies a group, non-allowlisted sender, old/repeated item, oversized image, cross-account memory
  query, or late administrator confirmation;
• recording reaches its free-space floor, loses one sensor, restarts a camera, or recovers an interrupted session;
• autonomy loses its sensor or leaves its radius;
• an AI tool proposes an unsupported or stale action;
• physical E-stop is pressed at each declared operating speed and pose.



Measure stop behavior
For each layer, measure detection time, command time, electrical response, mechanical response, and final state.
Report surface, slope, load, battery state, speed, controller mode, firmware/software revision, sample rate, and
uncertainty. “600 ms watchdog” is not the same as “stopped within 600 ms.”

    SAFETY CHECK

    Use rated stands, restraints, remote observation, current limiting, a spotter, and an independent stop before
    intentionally injecting failures. Some failures should be tested only in simulation or on surrogate hardware.


    NO SAFETY CERTIFICATION

    No complete hazard analysis, requirements matrix, measured stopping-distance set, thermal/current report,
    slope/flipper/arm qualification, or qualified engineering sign-off was supplied. Eight years of experiments and
    demonstrations are valuable historical evidence but are not a certification. Publication of this history does not
    approve ROB for public operation or approve a copy for construction. Any live event must create and sign its
    own current risk assessment before motion.




ORBITUS ROBOTICS - FIRST MAKER FAIRE EDITION                                                                     64 / 220
CHAPTER 14


Maker Faire operations
CHAPTER 14


Present ROB safely in public
Roles
Assign at least these responsibilities:

• Lead operator: owns arm/disarm and the primary controller.
• Safety spotter: watches the robot and crowd, keeps a hand near the physical E-stop, and may stop without
  asking.
• Educator: explains ROB without distracting the operator.
• Technical support: monitors power, temperature, logs, and recovery.
• Event liaison: coordinates space, charging, barriers, accessibility, and emergency requirements.

One person may fill multiple roles only if workload and risk allow it. Never make the operator answer a long technical
question while ROB moves near visitors.



Pre-show checklist
   current approved software/firmware versions recorded;
   batteries inspected, charged under the approved procedure, and restrained;
   fuses, disconnect, and E-stop visually inspected and function-tested;
   treads, chain, sprockets, guards, shafts, collars, mounts, fasteners, and torque marks inspected;
   arm, neck, flipper, and actuator limits checked in a protected area;
   controllers uniquely paired; unused credentials revoked;
   control ownership, per-arm authority, and all enabled video feeds tested under venue network conditions;
   speed cap and operating mode verified on every controller;
   barrier, route, slope, floor transitions, cables, exits, and charging location inspected;
   camera, microphone, face recognition, Messages, transcript memory, recording, location, and cloud-AI policy
   displayed;
   fire response, first aid, lift/transport, and fault-recovery plans briefed;
   spare tools and parts controlled so visitors cannot reach them;
   dry rehearsal completed with the full team.



Show-state model
Use explicit visible states: Transport Safe, Display Only, Demo Armed, Moving, Stopped, Faulted, Charging, and
Maintenance. Each transition names who authorizes it and what checklist applies. Reconnecting a controller or
restarting software never silently arms motion.




                                                            66
BUILDING R.O.B.                                                                          COMPLETE FIELD MANUAL - ADVANCED



Audience and privacy
Keep an accessible viewing route. Do not require visitors to step over cables. Mark pinch and tread boundaries
clearly. Avoid publishing or enrolling faces without consent. When camera, microphone, face recognition, Mes-
sages, transcript memory, recording, or cloud processing is active, use plain-language signage and a visible indica-
tor; provide a quick disable/delete path and a non-recorded, non-enrolled way to participate. A recognized identity
is personalization only and never event admission, controller ownership, or action authority.

    EVENT PLAN IS VENUE-SPECIFIC

   The book records Maker Faire history, not a reusable show permit. Barrier dimensions, permitted speed, staff,
   E-stop placement, charging, lifting, transport, insurance, consent signage, demonstration script, weather re-
   sponse, and abort criteria must be completed for the actual venue and current robot. Until that signed event
   packet exists, ROB should remain static or operate only in a separately approved controlled test area.

CHAPTER 15


Maintenance and change control
CHAPTER 15


Keep the robot
understandable
Before and after every operating day
Inspect for loose fasteners, moved torque marks, chain and tread damage, shaft-collar movement, bracket cracks or
bending, wire abrasion, connector heating, battery swelling or damage, fluid or debris, blocked ventilation, actuator
looseness, unusual sound, camera security, E-stop function, and log errors.
After operation, record runtime, surfaces, mode, maximum observed current and temperature if available, incidents,
unexpected stops, repairs, and deferred defects. Quarantine the robot from powered use when a red condition
remains unresolved.



Scheduled maintenance record
For every interval, name the component, task, acceptable condition, tool, replacement part, torque or measurement,
evidence, and sign-off. Intervals may be hours, cycles, calendar time, or before each public show.



Configuration control
A change to code, pin mapping, motor polarity, brake logic, network framing, controller speed cap, arm limit, bat-
tery arrangement, or bracket can invalidate earlier tests. Give hardware, firmware, software, calibration, wiring, and
operating procedure separate revision identifiers, then record the tested combination.



Troubleshooting by layer
                            SAFE DIAGNOSTIC PATH

   NO APPROVED MAINTENANCE SCHEDULE

   The archive documents repairs and revisions but not a complete approved maintenance program. It supplies
   no authoritative wear limits, torque schedule, battery retirement criteria, calibration interval, spares list, or
   released restore image. A future operating edition must derive those items from component documentation,
   measurements, failure history, and current inspection. This historical edition does not convert age or continued
   operation into a maintenance interval.

   WHY THE COMPLETE MANUAL INCLUDES THE FULL ADVANCED VOLUMES

   The hardware chapters above establish ROB’s physical plant, evidence status, and operating boundaries. The
   following four manuscripts preserve the complete software and arm tutorials beside that record: AI-assisted
   engineering practice, AMBER/Ubuntu/CAN/LCM, the Vision Pro controller, and Cerebro’s mixed-language per-
   ception/model/video architecture. They are included as real instructional content, not page-count filler. When
   a repeated concept appears, read the subsystem volume for code-level detail and the earlier field-manual
   chapter for the whole-robot safety boundary.

AI-assisted robotics engineering




                73
    CHAPTER 16


    Building R.O.B.
    AI, Robotics, and the Codex-Accelerated Evolution of
    ROBController and Cerebro
    A source-based field guide
    Expanded repository-integration edition, August 23, 2026




    Preface: one builder, many keyboards
    R.O.B. did not begin as an “AI-written robot.” It began the normal way: one person, several computers, physical
    hardware, partial ideas, experiments that worked once, experiments that broke connectors, and commits made
    before a fragile discovery disappeared.
    The fresh v5 and current Git record uses three author-name spellings—Orbitus, RudyAramayo, and Rudy Aramayo—but
    the same orbitus@orbitusrobotics.com email. The older archive also records Rob Makina, Rodolfo Aramayo, and
    one placeholder you@example.com identity. Those labels are consistent with a long-lived solo project moving among
    differently configured computers; they are not by themselves a complete contributor biography. Nor can Git prove
    which lines were suggested by an AI. Commit metadata records the person who accepted and committed a change,
    not every tool involved in producing it.
    Still, the history has a visible hinge. Through September 2025, commits read like a lab notebook: tune a servo, repair
    a port, checkpoint an animation, avoid sending commands too quickly. Beginning August 1, 2026, the repositories
    gain coordinated transport protocols, certificate pinning, Keychain-backed pairing, formal message schemas, fix-
    ture tests, failure boundaries, and long operator documents. ROBController’s first large 2026 change adds roughly
    5,345 lines and Cerebro’s adds roughly 16,478. That is the point at which the work looks AI-accelerated.
    This book treats August 1, 2026 as the start of the Codex-accelerated era. That is an interpretation based on scale
    and engineering form, not an authorship claim. Rudy remains the author and operator: the person choosing the
    goal, checking the diff, testing the hardware, and assuming responsibility for what the robot does.




    1. The robot is a distributed system
             SOURCE TRAIL — ANALYZING NOW: use Cerebro’s main Objective-C++ view controller, ROBCon-
         troller’s ConsciousViewController.mm, and ROBControllerVision’s ContentView.swift. These are three en-
         try points into one distributed robot. OPEN-SOURCE-CODE-MAP.md prints the exact full paths.

    R.O.B. is best understood as two cooperating products.
    ROBController is the operator edge. It runs on Apple mobile devices, accepts human intent, shows state, relays
    Watch commands, and acts as the approval console. Cerebro is the robot-side macOS application. It owns percep-
    tion, speech, AI sessions, serial hardware, Python helpers, arm scripts, autonomy, stage shows, and the server side
    of the control link.
    The important flow is:
1    human / Apple Watch
2            |


                                                             74
    BUILDING R.O.B.                                                                           COMPLETE FIELD MANUAL - ADVANCED


3                v
4     ROBController -- authenticated intent --> Cerebro -- bounded commands --> hardware
5                ^                                  |
6                |                                  +--> cameras / lidar / speech / AI
7                +---------- status and results ----+


    This architecture implies a rule that should govern every future change:

              Generative AI may propose intent; deterministic software and an accountable operator decide
            whether that intent can become physical motion.

    Robot code has consequences that ordinary application code does not. A UI bug is inconvenient. A stale nonzero
    tread command, a wrong joint sign, or an unbounded action queue can injure someone or damage hardware. The
    mature code therefore does more than add intelligence. It constrains intelligence.




    2. Handmade foundations: Cerebro 2017–2025 and
    controller 2022–2023
               SOURCE TRAIL — HISTORICAL ARCHIVE: CEREBRO_ARCHIVE_HISTORY.md records the preserved
            Cerebro v1 through Cerebro v5 folders, the pre-Git file fingerprints, and the old Git coordinates. The
            archive folders are evidence sources, not public clone instructions.

    The present public Cerebro history is not the beginning of Cerebro. It is the history of a fresh repository created
    around an existing robot application. The five preserved archive folders restore the missing prologue.

    • v1, ROB additions dated 2017-2018: a Cocoa Arduino serial example became a Mac command station for
      ROB’s seven-field motor frame.
    • v2, 2018: speech, Base/Head/Torso serial roles, SceneKit, multipeer control, depth/user-tracking attempts, a
      named consciousness bridge, and Leap Motion entered the first surviving Git history.
    • v3, 2018-2022: controller arbitration, autonomy permission, servos, multilingual speech, RealSense/ReSpeaker
      processes, human/head tracking, RTSP, T265, AutoNet, and the RPLidar split accumulated across the historical
      branches.
    • v4, 2018-2025 lineage: the older history reached Apple silicon, Google LLM responses, singleton-process
      work, continuous speech, and Foundation Models experiments.
    • v5, fresh root in 2025: the already substantial program entered a new repository and continued toward the
      current system.

    The v1 folder must be read carefully. Its Cocoa shell preserves Gabe Ghearing’s 2009 “Arduino Serial Example”
    attribution. That is upstream code ancestry, not a claim that ROB or Cerebro began in 2009. ROB-specific headers
    credit Rob Makina in September 2017 and January 2018, the Xcode product is named Cerebro, and the adapted
    source opens a fixed USB device at 250000 baud. It exposes forward, reverse, turn, flipper, and linear-actuator ac-
    tions using the same seven signed fields that survive in ROB’s historical 42-byte frame. Keyboard and peer-network
    classes show the first attempt to let more than one interface reach that physical command path.
    The v2 Git repository begins with 0141a646 on 1 January 2018. Within a day, its commits add speech, separate
    Base/Head/Torso serial paths, bound Mac controls, SceneKit, and multipeer communication. January commits
    bring in OpenNI, PCL, and NiTE experiments; one celebrates point-cloud data appearing in Cerebro and another
    plainly records a failed integration. May adds further libfreenect work and Leap Motion. The main controller al-
    ready composes speech, serial hardware, keyboard control, a 3D view, peer communication, perception, chat, and
    a class named ROBConsciousness. The name documents intent, not a claim that the early consciousness bridge was
    complete.


    ORBITUS ROBOTICS - FIRST MAKER FAIRE EDITION                                                                   75 / 220
    BUILDING R.O.B.                                                                          COMPLETE FIELD MANUAL - ADVANCED


    The v3 archive continues the 2018 root. A May 2019 commit requires a master controller identity to prevent con-
    flicting input and permission before autonomous mode. Later commits record Maestro servo noise, volume and
    mood, multilingual speech, wireless joining, head tracking, ReSpeaker and RealSense task processes, human track-
    ing, RTSP, T265, headless-performance work, and repaired speech/chat behavior. Its master ends in April 2022
    with AutoNet, iPad controller binding, and the decision to split RPLidar into another repository.
    The v4 archive preserves a related branch of the same 2018 history through July 2025. It records an M1 checkpoint,
    memory-safe text output, USB networking, a Google-LLM demo, a singleton-process check, continuous-speech
    experiments, and Foundation Models work. One July 2025 commit explicitly says the builder is creating the next v5
    release. The independent v5 root on 5 August therefore marks a migration, not an inception.
    This recovered history changes how the modern code should be read. Cerebro’s roots are the practical needs of a
    physical machine: format a frame, control three embedded roles, hear a person, speak back, arbitrate controllers,
    and experiment with imperfect sensors without losing the ability to drive. The newer protocols and AI systems refine
    boundaries that the workshop had already been discovering for years.
    The controller has its own surviving foundation. ROBController’s 2022 root commit established the controller around
    AutoNet and ROBONet after earlier ML-model experiments were removed. It mixed Objective-C, Objective-C++,
    Swift, C, and C++, plus Apple’s AurioTouch sample-derived audio machinery. This was a practical choice: keep the
    old hardware-facing code and add modern Apple code where it helped.
    In September 2023, the controller gained a custom iPhone storyboard, text commands, lidar visualization, speech-
    recognition work, system-volume tests, TCP no-delay tuning, layout cleanup, and an early Watch app. These
    changes are recognizably exploratory. UI, network behavior, and speech lifecycle were being discovered together.


    What Codex should learn from this era
    Do not begin a legacy-robot task by rewriting the language mix. First ask Codex to map ownership and data flow:
1     Inspect ROBController without changing files. Trace a command from the iPhone
2     or Watch UI through AutoNet serialization to the network send. Identify the
3     Objective-C/Swift boundary, thread or queue transitions, and every place a
4     stale command can survive. Cite files and line numbers. Mark uncertainty.


    The useful output is a map, not code. Once the map is reviewed, request the smallest change that proves the model
    correct. This protects working legacy behavior while Codex builds context.




    3. Era two: the v5 migration and solo workshop,
    August–September 2025
    Cerebro’s fresh repository begins August 5, 2025, with the parentless commit 4c4f1d4: “initial commit with v5
    changes and a fresh repo.” Its initial tree is already a substantial robot program because it imports the earlier
    workshop, not because that program appeared in one day. It includes a macOS storyboard, camera management,
    speech, serial control, AutoNet, lidar, Kinect/RealSense-era components, Leap Motion, RTSP video, task launchers,
    Core ML assets, and a large body of mixed Objective-C++ and Swift.
    The following weeks read like a maker’s engineering diary:

    • Gemini is connected, speech delays are adjusted, and OAK camera input begins.
    • Camera devices become discoverable at runtime.
    • A new upper-neck servo is introduced and calibrated.
    • RPLidar launch paths and wake-word handling are repaired.
    • Output language propagates from controller to robot voice.
    • Amber arm v1/v2 scripts are integrated, corrected, and exercised against real L10 and R11 arms.

    ORBITUS ROBOTICS - FIRST MAKER FAIRE EDITION                                                                  76 / 220
    BUILDING R.O.B.                                                                            COMPLETE FIELD MANUAL - ADVANCED


    • Keyframes and dual-arm sequences grow from manual experiments.
    • Vision person segmentation, 3D body pose, SceneKit skeletons, and whole-body overlays are added.
    • Speech wordiness, “shut up,” camera toggling, and head tracking become operator features.

    The commit messages are valuable technical evidence. One warns that arm values are “very scary if the values go
    wrong.” Another records a damaged CAN connector. Another checkpoints code before rebuilding a pose view
    on every camera frame. These are not embarrassing details. They express the central truth of robotics: hardware
    provides the final test, and the cost of a bad assumption can be physical.


    Turning workshop knowledge into specifications
    Before asking Codex to touch hardware code, translate lived knowledge into invariants:
1     Goal: add a head-tilt command.
2

3     Constraints:
4     - Never emit a value outside the calibrated channel-2 range.
5     - A missing device must disable only head tilt.
6     - Manual stop must preempt queued motion.
7     - Preserve existing torso and camera behavior.
8     - Add a pure serialization fixture before editing the serial path.
9     - Do not claim hardware validation; give me an exact on-robot checklist.


    This prompt is good because it tells Codex what must remain true. It does not dictate an implementation before
    the repository has been inspected.




    4. The acceleration hinge: August 1, 2026
    The paired August 1 commits transform both applications.
    In ROBController, the large change introduces secure v2 transport, the Watch companion path, action and Watch
    command protocols, approval/status surfaces, autonomy controls, tests, and documentation.
    In Cerebro, the corresponding change introduces the server side of the secure control plane, Gemini Robotics Live
    integration, an autonomy coordinator, camera H.264 transport for Vision Pro, a supervised Python runtime, system
    dependency management, action/video protocols, extensive tests, and a major documentation set.
    This is the maturity jump. The old system primarily moved bytes and trusted its environment. The new system names
    roles, versions messages, authenticates peers, bounds queues, records operator intent, and fails closed.


    Why this resembles AI-assisted engineering
    No single clue proves AI use. Together, these clues make acceleration a reasonable inference:

    1. Change size increases by an order of magnitude.
    2. Both sides of a distributed protocol evolve together.
    3. Production code arrives with fixture and integration tests.
    4. Threat-model concerns—pinning, replay resistance, downgrade prevention, revocation, role checks—are ad-
       dressed systematically.
    5. Operator documentation explains contracts and incomplete capabilities.
    6. Later commits use structured conventional-commit language and detailed implementation summaries.

    The right conclusion is not “AI replaced the programmer.” It is “the builder acquired a fast engineering collaborator,
    and the repository began to preserve more of the reasoning that previously lived only in the builder’s head.”



    ORBITUS ROBOTICS - FIRST MAKER FAIRE EDITION                                                                    77 / 220
    BUILDING R.O.B.                                                                          COMPLETE FIELD MANUAL - ADVANCED



    5. Rebuilding the secure control plane with Codex
                SOURCE TRAIL — ANALYZING NOW: compare the identically named protocol files in ROBCon-
            troller’s AutoNetClient and Cerebro’s AutoNetShared. Then read the transport-security and integration
            fixtures. The code map gives every exact filename and full path.

    The v2 control plane is the strongest example of how to use Codex on a cross-repository change.


    5.1 Start with a contract
    Ask for a design before edits:
1     Analyze ROBController and Cerebro together. Design a versioned replacement for
2     the plaintext robot-control path. Requirements: QUIC with TLS 1.3, exact server
3     certificate pinning, per-device pairing secrets in Keychain, reciprocal proof
4     of possession, controller and lidar roles, persistent revocation, no automatic
5     legacy downgrade, bounded framing, and deterministic fixture tests. Identify
6     the files on both sides and list migration risks. Do not edit yet.


    Review the design for four separations:

    • Identity: which Cerebro instance is this?
    • Pairing: has this device been enrolled?
    • Authorization: is it an operator or telemetry publisher?
    • Freshness: is this proof from the current connection rather than a replay?

    Only then authorize implementation in checkpoints.


    5.2 Implement the shared wire format first
    Message formats should have explicit magic bytes, versions, lengths, limits, and error behavior. Generate the same
    golden fixtures on client and server. Codex is especially useful here because it can compare two implementations
    and find drift.
1     Implement only the v2 frame codec and fixtures in both repositories. Do not
2     open sockets and do not modify UI. Reject unknown mandatory versions, oversized
3     payloads, truncated frames, and invalid role/action combinations. Run the
4     fixture tests and show the exact diff.




    5.3 Add transport without weakening authentication
    QUIC/TLS encrypts a connection, but encryption alone does not tell a controller that it reached its robot. ROB-
    Controller pins Cerebro’s certificate. Pairing then proves possession of a separate per-device secret, while Cerebro
    resolves the device role from its own registry.
    The August 10 fixes demonstrate why network handshakes need state-machine tests. iOS did not always expose
    Bonjour TXT metadata, and both peers could wait for the other to speak. The repair adds a padded pairing hello
    before the server sends its challenge, validates the controller ID, and preserves pinning and reciprocal proof.
    A productive debugging prompt is:
1     Diagnose the QUIC pairing stall using both repositories. Draw the client and
2     server state machines from connection start through authenticated readiness.
3     Find any state where both peers await input. Preserve certificate pinning,
4     device-role lookup, and reciprocal proof. Add a regression fixture for the
5     deadlock before changing production code.




    ORBITUS ROBOTICS - FIRST MAKER FAIRE EDITION                                                                  78 / 220
    BUILDING R.O.B.                                                                             COMPLETE FIELD MANUAL - ADVANCED


    Never “fix” a handshake by skipping verification or silently falling back to plaintext. Availability bugs must not be-
    come authentication bugs.




    6. Operator authority, Watch control, and autonomy
    ROBController becomes more than a joystick in the mature system. It relays Watch intent, advertises accepted action
    capabilities, approves proposed robot actions, and starts a bounded autonomy session.
    The autonomy model is intentionally a session, not indefinite permission. The social_roam profile captures an activa-
    tion pose, stays within a radius, uses fresh lidar, moves slowly, and ends on manual control, explicit stop, or Cerebro
    restart. Meanwhile ROBSerialBox treats a controller snapshot as stale when its local receipt age exceeds 0.6 seconds.
    That interval is roughly three expected 5 Hz publications, but the implementation is time based rather than a fixed
    missed-message count. It sends one neutral/braked frame, then stops USB writes so the Arduino deadman can
    de-energize the treads.
    This yields a useful authority hierarchy:
1     emergency / stop intent
2                > direct operator control
3                > bounded approved autonomy
4                > AI proposal
5                > idle


    Ask Codex to encode this hierarchy in tests, not just comments:
1     Add table-driven tests for authority arbitration. Stop must preempt every
2     state. Manual control must end autonomy. Restart must default to autonomy off.
3     An expired controller snapshot must produce exactly one neutral/braked write
4     and no continuing heartbeat. Do not add arm movement.


    The final sentence matters. Mature agents are capable of expanding a coherent feature beyond its safe scope. Tell
    Codex which adjacent capability is explicitly excluded.




    7. Vision, depth, and video as bounded pipelines
               SOURCE TRAIL — ANALYZING NOW: CameraManager.swift owns separate face/belly RGB-D delivery,
            the ROBInsta360 services own the panoramic path, and ROBVideoServer.swift owns three-feed video
            serving. Vision Pro receivers and flat/immersive presentation live in ROBControllerVision’s video feature
            and platform directories.

    Cerebro’s camera path evolves from selecting webcams to a multi-provider perception system.
    The 2026 implementation runs each OAK role in a supervised Python helper and sends synchronized RGB and
    aligned depth over a private Unix socket. Face and belly cameras have distinct MXIDs, sockets, lifecycle state,
    and on-device pipelines. Keeping the SDK outside Cerebro isolates USB disconnects, malformed packets, missing
    packages, and Python crashes. AVFoundation remains an RGB fallback, but only one provider may own a physical
    OAK device. A third headless service controls and decodes Insta360 Pro II panoramic video on demand.
    The separate Vision Pro service exposes independent front, belly, and insta360 H.264 pipelines on its own authen-
    ticated QUIC connection. Each feed has bounded send state and newest-frame admission. Slow video cannot back
    up robot control. Dropped frames trigger keyframe recovery rather than an unbounded queue, and the panoramic
    feed can move from a flat panel to an inward-facing mixed-immersive sphere without preserving motion authority.
    The reusable design lesson is separate by failure domain:

    • control and video use different services and queues;

    ORBITUS ROBOTICS - FIRST MAKER FAIRE EDITION                                                                     79 / 220
     BUILDING R.O.B.                                                                            COMPLETE FIELD MANUAL - ADVANCED


     • Python SDK faults do not terminate Cerebro;
     • camera ownership is exclusive;
     • camera role, calibration, rate budget, and demand are explicit;
     • perception drops old work instead of accumulating latency;
     • an unavailable optional device degrades one capability.

     Codex prompt:
1      Trace camera frames from provider to Vision, Gemini, and Vision Pro. For each
2      consumer record queue, ownership, backpressure, timestamp, and failure policy.
3      Then add the smallest change that keeps only the newest pending frame for the
4      slow consumer. Prove robot-control queues are untouched. Add malformed IPC and
5      slow-consumer tests; separate simulator evidence from hardware validation.




     8. AI inside Cerebro: proposals, tools, and speech
                 SOURCE TRAIL — ANALYZING NOW: Cerebro/Cerebro/ROBAI.swift is the optional AI integration,
             while Cerebro/Cerebro/GeminiRoboticsProtocol.swift defines bounded high-level proposals. Search
             callers before assuming that a declared action has a physical executor.

     The repositories currently document Gemini Robotics Live as the runtime model integration. OpenAI enters this
     story in two different ways:

     1. Codex develops and maintains the codebase.
     2. The OpenAI Responses API can power a future model-neutral robot brain.

     These roles should not be conflated. A coding agent may edit a protocol while an application model later uses that
     protocol as a tool.
     For an OpenAI-backed adapter, preserve Cerebro’s deterministic boundaries. The model receives observations
     and returns structured proposals. Cerebro validates the schema, allow-list, session, deadline, and current authority.
     ROBController approves where required. Only a deterministic executor may touch hardware.
     Conceptual Swift-like pseudocode:
1      let proposal = try await openAI.responses.create(
2           model: configuredModel,
3           input: currentRobotContext,
4           tools: [robotActionProposalSchema]
5      )
6

7      let action = try RobotActionProtocol.validate(proposal)
8      try authority.requireActiveSession(action.sessionID)
9      try policy.requireAllowed(action)
10     await operatorConsole.requestApproval(action)


     Use the Responses API for multi-turn reasoning and tool calling, and choose the model through configuration rather
     than scattering a model name through UI and network code. Keep credentials outside source control. Do not send
     secrets, pairing material, faces, audio, or private surroundings unless the product’s data policy and operator consent
     explicitly allow it.
     The current Cerebro implementation makes that privacy boundary concrete in two new subsystems. The face rec-
     ognizer keeps consented embeddings and samples encrypted on the Mac and contributes only expiring untrusted
     scene context; WebFace4M and VGGFace2 profiles remain model-specific. The Messages bridge is disabled by
     default, exact-sender allowlisted, one-to-one only, at-most-once, and stripped of motor, file, Music, device, camera,


     ORBITUS ROBOTICS - FIRST MAKER FAIRE EDITION                                                                    80 / 220
    BUILDING R.O.B.                                                                             COMPLETE FIELD MANUAL - ADVANCED


    and microphone tools. Its optional encrypted transcript memory is sender/account scoped; when Gemini is used,
    selected text excerpts leave the Mac and that disclosure must be explicit. Neither “administrator” face identity nor
    conversational memory is authorization.


    A safe OpenAI integration task for Codex
1     Add an OpenAI Responses API provider behind the existing model-neutral
2     improvisation/proposal interface. First inspect the current official OpenAI
3     documentation. Keep the existing provider as default. Read the API key from a
4     secure runtime source, never source or logs. Return only the existing strict
5     schema. No model output may directly invoke serial, shell, network-control, or
6     arm code. Add mocked success, timeout, malformed output, refusal, and
7     cancellation tests. Do not enable the provider automatically.


    The official OpenAI model guidance recommends the Responses API for reasoning, tool use, and multi-turn work-
    flows. Models and capabilities change; let Codex consult current official documentation during implementation
    instead of freezing this book’s August 2026 model names into the architecture.




    9. Local improvisation and stage shows
    The August 3 Cerebro change adds a safe local-improvisation layer, a connection-tolerant show coordinator, diag-
    nostics, a llama.cpp provider, strict show schemas, fixture tests, and a Maker Faire opening show.
    This feature demonstrates layered trust:

    • authored dialogue is the mandatory fallback;
    • local generation may select only allow-listed beats and delivery;
    • Gemini may add live context to a trusted brief;
    • show files cannot contain raw joints, servo values, hosts, ports, or shell;
    • uncalibrated gestures fail closed;
    • expired stage contexts permanently reject non-stop tool calls;
    • stop_motion remains available through a priority stop lane.

    Generative language becomes safe not because the model is guaranteed to behave, but because untrusted output
    has a narrow grammar and a deterministic fallback.
    When asking Codex to extend a show format, request the validator and negative fixtures before the runner:
1     Extend robshow v1 with an optional audience-response beat. The JSON may select
2     only a named response policy and timeout. It may not contain executable code,
3     raw motor values, file paths, hosts, ports, or arbitrary tool names. Update the
4     schema, parser, negative fixtures, dry-run renderer, and docs before enabling
5     runtime behavior. Preserve authored fallback and stop preemption.




    Interlude: the August 14–23 integration sprint
    The ten days after the original local-model edition changed the unit of work. Instead of one isolated feature at a time,
    the repositories began moving in cross-system slices whose contracts had to agree across the Mac, iPhone/iPad,
    Vision Pro, lidar publisher, camera helpers, and AMBER runtime.
    The material changes include:

    ORBITUS ROBOTICS - FIRST MAKER FAIRE EDITION                                                                     81 / 220
    BUILDING R.O.B.                                                                           COMPLETE FIELD MANUAL - ADVANCED


    • an always-on, singleton Cerebro process with wake recovery and crash-limited supervision;
    • separate face, belly, and headless Insta360 camera roles, selectable detectors, per-camera budgets, and a
      system-status dashboard;
    • three authenticated H.264 feeds and a physically aligned mixed-immersive panorama;
    • explicit Request/Release Control arbitration between phone and Vision clients;
    • independent left/right rob-arm-control/2 authority and simultaneous bounded PSVR Sense joint jogging;
    • destination navigation, sidewalk segmentation, stale-sensor gates, and manually confirmed traversability learn-
      ing;
    • compact RLS1 lidar frames, authenticated same-Mac IPC with QUIC fallback, pose-aligned maps, .robomap per-
      sistence, and map/base-layer calibration;
    • explicit synchronized recording for RGB, lossless depth, stereo, lidar, calibration, pose, odometry, authority, la-
      bels, and separate camera movies;
    • a disabled-by-default, allowlisted one-to-one Messages AI bridge with bounded images, local current-
      information tools, optional encrypted transcript memory, and exact-confirmation local administrator scripts; and
    • a disabled-by-default consented face gallery with local encrypted enrollment and selectable AdaFace Web-
      Face4M/VGGFace2 encoders.

    The strongest architectural result is not any individual model. It is the repeated separation of identity, memory,
    observation, operator ownership, and physical authority. A face label can personalize a greeting but cannot ap-
    prove a command. A Messages sender can enter a narrow allowlisted conversation or exact administrator-command
    path but cannot inherit robot tools. A recorded autonomous command can document a run but cannot label its own
    ground truth. A panoramic window can survive a scene transition while drive is independently braked.
    This is also a lesson in evidence. The Volume 5 Change Atlas now records the commit families across all affected
    repositories. The August 23 snapshot notes that ROBControllerVision’s latest three panorama-orientation commits
    were clean but still ahead of its public remote. “Implemented locally” and “reproducible from the public branch”
    are different claims, and a good AI collaborator should surface that distinction before publication.




    10. Dependencies are part of robot safety
    Earlier Cerebro code contained machine-specific paths and assumed tools were installed. The mature version treats
    dependencies as fallible inputs.
    Python can be selected, auto-detected, or created in an app-managed virtual environment. Package installation
    occurs only after an operator action. sshpass, ticcmd, and RPLidar are resolved and validated before launch. Miss-
    ing optional tools disable their feature rather than crash Cerebro. Privileged MacPorts installation is shown to the
    operator in Terminal; the app does not capture an administrator password. The SSH password travels through an
    anonymous pipe rather than the process argument list.
    This is an excellent Codex refactoring pattern:
1     Inventory every Process/NSTask launch in Cerebro. Do not edit yet. Classify the
2     executable as required or optional; list path source, validation, credentials,
3     timeout, restart behavior, and user-visible failure. Then centralize only the
4     shared preflight while preserving each feature's behavior. Add tests for
5     missing, directory, non-executable, and disappearing executables.


    Codex can search every launch site more reliably than a tired human, but the operator must decide which depen-
    dency failures are tolerable.




    ORBITUS ROBOTICS - FIRST MAKER FAIRE EDITION                                                                   82 / 220
    BUILDING R.O.B.                                                                         COMPLETE FIELD MANUAL - ADVANCED



    11. A repeatable Codex workflow for these reposito-
    ries
    Step 1: protect the worktree
    Start every session with:
1     Inspect git status in ROBController and Cerebro. Existing changes belong to me.
2     Do not overwrite, revert, stage, or commit them. Identify generated Xcode user
3     state separately. Summarize the relevant architecture before editing.


    The current worktrees, for example, contain modified UserInterfaceState.xcuserstate files. They are user state,
    not permission to discard anything.


    Step 2: state outcome and invariants
    Prefer “When control packets stop, the base brakes once and becomes silent” to “refactor ROBSerialBox.” The
    former is testable and safety-relevant.


    Step 3: ask for read-only diagnosis
    For cross-language and cross-repository behavior, have Codex trace the entire path before implementation. Ask it
    to cite evidence and say what it cannot know without hardware.


    Step 4: divide at stable boundaries
    Good checkpoints are codec, fixtures, client state machine, server state machine, UI, and documentation. Avoid
    one request that changes wire format, physical actuation, and user experience without intermediate review.


    Step 5: make Codex edit and verify
1     Implement the approved codec checkpoint. Preserve unrelated changes. Run the
2     narrow fixture tests first, then the relevant build. Review your own diff for
3     security, concurrency, compatibility, and accidental secrets. Report commands,
4     results, remaining hardware checks, and files changed.




    Step 6: inspect the diff yourself
    Use git diff --check, git diff --stat, and git diff. Pay special attention to entitlements, Info.plist permissions,
    Xcode project membership, networking, Keychain accessibility, dispatch queues, and motor values.


    Step 7: validate in layers
    1. Pure codec/schema fixtures
    2. Unit tests with mocked time and I/O
    3. Client/server integration tests on loopback
    4. Xcode build and static diagnostics
    5. Simulator/UI checks where meaningful
    6. Bench hardware with motors disabled or lifted
    7. Low-speed supervised robot test with an accessible stop

    Codex may complete layers one through five. It must never imply that they prove layers six and seven.

    ORBITUS ROBOTICS - FIRST MAKER FAIRE EDITION                                                                 83 / 220
    BUILDING R.O.B.                                                                      COMPLETE FIELD MANUAL - ADVANCED


    Step 8: commit the reasoning
    A mature message explains behavior and risk:
1     fix(network): prevent QUIC pairing handshake deadlock
2

3     - send a bounded client hello before awaiting the challenge
4     - validate device identity against the paired registry
5     - preserve certificate pinning and reciprocal proof
6     - add a regression fixture for simultaneous-read startup


    The history then becomes usable context for the next human or AI session.




    12. Prompt recipes for the actual change families
    Cross-repository protocol change
1     Trace protocol X in both repos. Produce a compatibility matrix for old client,
2     new client, old server, and new server. Design golden fixtures before edits.
3     Reject malformed and oversized input. No automatic security downgrade. Make
4     changes in reviewable checkpoints and run both test suites.




    Concurrency bug
1     Build a state machine from code, queues, callbacks, and cancellation paths.
2     Identify races, double completion, and mutual waits. Add a deterministic
3     regression test. Keep UI updates on the main actor/queue and network state on
4     its owning queue. Do not mask the bug with sleeps.




    Camera crash
