 ledger
 This appendix is the development queue for a future dimensionally complete technical edition. The present histor-
 ical edition does not need to invent these values. Each future entry should be supported by a dated measurement,
 drawing, test record, authorized public reference, or reviewed first-person account.

 1. Expand the first-person project chronology with dated photographs, receipts, and workshop records that relate
    the circa-2016 College Station treaded prototype to the now-documented 2017-2025 Cerebro source history
    and later physical revisions.

    environmental limits.

    PPE, and vendor sequence.

    performance tests.

    gauges, connectors, and power budget.

    timing, IMU calibration, IR positions/status, and speed-feedback status.

    ship, and dependency versions.
10. Arm and neck models, geometry, calibration, zero/home, soft/hard limits, payload, current/torque limits, colli-
    sion envelope, and safe transport.

    lighting, and present host path.
12. Controller compatibility, pairing/revocation, speed caps, latency, watchdogs, location-data policy, and Vision

    camera/microphone/cloud consent.
14. Hazard analysis, protective measures, E-stop proof, test reports, Maker Faire perimeter and staffing, fire, lifting,
    and charging plans, plus insurance and venue requirements.
15. Final photo captions, permissions/releases, vendor-publication permissions, credits, and redacted screenshots.

                                                       215
CHAPTER G


Serial protocol reference
Historical base frame
 FIELD        MEANING                     EXAMPLE             CURRENT LIMIT / NOTE
 Start        Frame marker                ~                   One byte
 1            Left brake                  +0000               0 described as release; nonzero engage
 2            Left signed speed           +0100               Expected magnitude within 0-255; not clamped
 3            Right brake                 +0000               Same mapping
 4            Right signed speed          +0100               Mirrored physical direction polarity
 5            Flipper brake               +0000               Same mapping
 6            Flipper signed speed        +0000               Expected magnitude within 0-255; not clamped
 7            Actuator signed speed       +0000               Nominal -3200 to +3200; +3201 and -3201 clear
                                                              safe start, then command +3200 and -3200 respec-
                                                              tively

Fields are five ASCII characters and separated by six commas. Total historical frame length is 42 bytes. The current
decoder defects listed in Chapter 7 must be fixed before treating the format as safe. A replacement should add
explicit version, message type, bounded length, sequence, integrity check, and typed state transitions.

CHAPTER I


Primary technical references
Repository paths document ROB’s implementation. The following manufacturer and standards sources document
the component or protocol boundary. Capture a dated PDF/hash for any reference used to release hardware; web
pages can change.

 TOPIC                  PRIMARY REFERENCE
 LT2 platform           SuperDroid Robots, LT2 support and current configurable platform: https://www.superd
                        roidrobots.com/wiki-a/index_php/LT2_and_LT2-F_Robot_Support and https://www.su
                        perdroidrobots.com/store/product=1513. Current catalog options do not identify ROB’s
                        purchased revision.
 Arduino Mega           Arduino Mega 2560 Rev3 hardware page, pinout, datasheet, and schematic: https://do
                        cs.arduino.cc/hardware/mega-2560.
 Arduino GIGA           Arduino GIGA R1 WiFi hardware page, pinout, datasheet, and schematic: https://docs
                        .arduino.cc/hardware/giga-r1-wifi.
 Motor serial           Pololu Simple Motor Controller User’s Guide, compact protocol and safe-start commands:
                        https://www.pololu.com/docs/0J44/all.
 Stepper control        Pololu Tic Stepper Motor Controller User’s Guide: https://www.pololu.com/docs/0J71/a
                        ll. Identify ROB’s exact Tic before applying ratings.
 Neck servo             Hitec HSB-9380TH product/specification page: https://hitecrcd.com/hsb-9380th-ult
                        ra-torque-brushless-titanium-gear-servo/.
 OAK cameras            Luxonis OAK-D Pro W hardware page and device-information example: https://docs.l
                        uxonis.com/hardware/products/OAK-D%20Pro%20W and https://docs.luxonis.com/softw
                        are-v3/depthai/examples/misc/device_information/.
 RPLidar                SLAMTEC product-specific datasheet and SDK selected after the installed model is iden-
                        tified; do not merge A1 and S2E interface claims.
 LCM                    Official LCM introduction and Python tutorial: https://lcm-proj.github.io/lcm/conten
                        t/tutorial.html and https://lcm-proj.github.io/lcm/content/tutorial-python.html.
 SocketCAN              Linux kernel SocketCAN documentation: https://docs.kernel.org/networking/can.htm
                        l.
 CAN physical layer     Texas Instruments, Controller Area Network Physical Layer Requirements, SLLA270; termi-
                        nation and grounding must also follow installed transceiver/vendor guidance.
 H.264 transport        IETF RFC 6184, RTP Payload Format for H.264 Video: https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/
                        rfc6184. ROB uses AVCC over custom reliable QUIC, not RTP; RFC 6184 is used to teach
                        NAL concepts and future RTP packetization distinctions.
 Apple H.264 decode     Apple Core Media, CMVideoFormatDescriptionCreateFromH264ParameterSets: https://de
                        veloper.apple.com/documentation/coremedia/cmvideoformatdescriptioncreatefromh264
                        parametersets.

The B-COMMAND and HengDrive drawings in the builder’s hardware folder remain controlled vendor records and
are not publication sources for this edition. Obtain written permission or an authorized public document before
reproducing or paraphrasing their drawings, tables, terminal details, ratings, or performance claims.

Photo and artwork credits
All construction and product/component photographs in this edition originate from the private Orbitus Robotics
gallery archive and were taken by Rodolfo Aramayo with his iPhone. ROB and the visible components are his prop-
erty, and there are no photographer or separately licensed product-photo exceptions in the closed publication
allowlist. The frontispiece and software-control illustrations are original generated artwork derived from ROB refer-
ence photographs and created for this book set. They contain no official franchise logo, character, or copied page
design and are labeled as illustrations rather than engineering evidence.
The book’s visual system uses licensed system typefaces and an original weathered retro-space workshop palette.
The project is independent and is not affiliated with or endorsed by any film studio, franchise owner, component
vendor, or platform company.
Any future third-party photograph, diagram, logo, or restricted document requires its own permission before publi-
cation. Prepared current-edition photographs are metadata-stripped and contain no identifiable person. The pub-
lisher must still accept the residual trademark and legal risk appropriate to the distribution plan without claiming
outside legal review.
